Apollo · mission control · session #245

Where the work stands

397 things are open. 203 of them are yours to rule, 194 are mine to build. 14 of the open items still have no checkable close condition — they are the frozen legacy set, and that set may only shrink.

6 of 8 gates were run just now and passed; 1 failed. The type-composite gate is not counted as a pass or a fail — it reports declared debt, which is a third thing.

The provenance gate is the one that is red, and it is red on purpose: 3 checks fail across 2 rulings that are missing a governs, evidence or status value. Those values are yours and nobody else’s — authoring them would be inventing provenance, so this page lists them and stops.

The order everything is shown in is a PROPOSAL — a score I compute from the store at build time, out of 5 weighted criteria printed in full below. It is not a ruling and it does not persist: you overrule it by writing a priority_override rank on the item, and an overruled item says DAVE OVERRULED wherever it appears. No item carries an override yet — the field is absent everywhere, because a priority I write into the store and then read back is not your judgement, it is mine wearing your name.

Flagged problem — 237 of 484 items carry any links (49%). The dependency graph the score's heaviest criterion (Unlock, weight 0.353) needs does not exist in the data, so every score on this page is flagged LOW CONFIDENCE and the unlock column reads zero for everything. This is MEASURED and QUEUED, not repaired: backfilling links would mean inventing the dependency graph and then scoring against my own invention. It is yours, and it is Friday 2026-08-14 work.

Everything on this page was read out of the stores or measured by running the thing. Nothing is asserted from memory.

Project — which body of work each item belongs to

383 Apollo, 101 Memento

These are my proposed defaults, not your ruling. You ruled that every item carries a stored project and that new items cannot be added without one; the values below are the assignment I proposed, written into _state.json for your eye. Change any of them by editing the item’s project in the store and regenerating — this page reports the store, it does not decide.

2 of them I could not call, and they say so. Anywhere those items appear, the word check sits next to the project label: W-14 (I put it in MEMENTO) — ⬛ DAVE'S FOUNDING PRINCIPLE; G12 (I put it in APOLLO) — Charter §4b tone-of-voice temperature map, PROVISIONAL 2026-07-02…. They are not marked by colour, and they are not marked as wrong — they are marked as MINE TO GUESS AND YOURS TO SETTLE.

Show Showing all 484 items.

The filter is a VIEW, not a state. It touches the board, the two plates and the priority table; it does not touch the stores, and it is not remembered — reload and you are back to all 484. Counts in the headings below re-count themselves when you filter, so a filtered heading never quotes an unfiltered total. Priority ranks do not renumber: rank 3 means third out of the whole backlog, and it keeps saying 3 in a filtered view, because a rank that changes with the view is not a rank.

SOURCE · knowledge/_state.json, field project, counted at generation. Legal values are the closed enum in knowledge/_state.py (apollo | memento), REQUIRED on every item and refused loudly when absent or misspelt. No project value is authored by this page.

Priority — a PROPOSAL, not a ruling

My proposed order, and the weights it came from

You ruled that I generate the priorities and you overrule them. This is the generated half. It is computed at build time from the store, never written back into it, so it cannot rot and it cannot quietly become a decision. 5 criteria, each scored 0–1, combined with the declared weights below into a score out of 100. The weights themselves are a proposal too — change any number in the table and the order changes; nothing about them is ratified.

There used to be a sixth criterion, and you removed it. “Effort (inverse)” carried 0.15 of the weight and, with no real effort values in the store, its only input was the BYTE LENGTH of an item’s body text — a proxy for how much had been written about a job, not for how big the job is. Dropped on your #168 review (DC2). The remaining weights are your original ratios — 30 : 20 : 15 : 10 : 10 — divided by their own sum, so every criterion stands in exactly the relationship to every other that it did before; only the removed 0.15 left the denominator. It has since come back as a CONDITIONAL criterion — the block under the table — but the byte-length proxy has not, and will not: the criterion now has one input, and no input means no criterion.

CriterionWeightWhat it measuresWhere the input comes from
Unlock0.353how much other work this item is blockingthe item's links array, plus inbound links from other items
Rot risk0.235cost of delay — how long it has been open, and whether it can even be closedopened (session number) and condition
Deadline0.176proximity to the Friday 2026-08-14 setthe item's OPTIONAL deadline (ISO date) where present — otherwise PROXY ONLY: a prose scan of title/body/closes_when.
Risk reduction0.118does closing this close a gate-coverage holePROXY ONLY — prose signal (gate / selftest / ratchet / coverage)
Decision relief0.118does finishing this take a decision OFF Dave's plateowner plus a prose scan for a pending decision
TOTAL1.000Score = 100 × Σ(weight × criterion). Deadline set: Friday 2026-08-14.

Effort is back — as a rung measured in TOKENS, and only where you have written one. The unit is real Claude tokens of job window (boot and wrap excluded) — the unit this project already rules and logs, not t-shirt sizes and not bytes of prose. Delegated sub spend is excluded too: a sub’s tokens are weekly quota, not this window’s fill, and they are not in the logged blocks these edges come from — so an L that needs a sub is a claim about this window, never a total bill. The three rungs mean this:

RungWhat it meansJob tokens, estimatedScores
Sfits beside other work in one window≤ 45,0001.00
Mis the window's job45,000 – 75,0000.50
Ldoes not fit one window — needs a lane or a sub> 75,0000.00

EDGES DERIVED, NOT TYPED — lower and upper quartile of the 26 token-priced session blocks in notes/_GAUGE-LOG.md (range 15,000–118,000 job tokens, median 63,693), rounded hard to the nearest 5,000 because 26 self-selected blocks cannot support a sharper edge. They move as the log grows; that is why they are printed rather than stored.

A rung is your ESTIMATE against a measured standard — it is not a measurement of the job, and the page will never print it as one. Writing "effort": "M" on an item is a forecast; the edges it is judged against are the measured history. Two more things worth knowing before you author any: (1) absent is legal and costs nothing — an item with no rung has no effort criterion at all, and its remaining weights renormalize, so it is scored on what it has rather than penalised for what it lacks; (2) a rung changes the whole row’s weighting — with a rung present, effort takes 0.15 and your other five snap back to their absolutes (Unlock 0.30 / Rot risk 0.20 / Deadline 0.15 / Risk reduction 0.10 / Decision relief 0.10). So a partly-authored store ranks two populations under two weightings; that is honest per item, but it is not a clean comparison across items. Today: 0 of 484 items carry a rung, so nothing below has moved. ⛔ No agent may author a value — every rung on this page will be yours. s168-D2 PENDING: the machinery is built and proven, the ruling is minted at the wrap.

Some inputs are proxies, and the table says which. opened, condition and owner are real fields. deadline is an OPTIONAL real field that replaces its proxy when present — today it is present on 1 of 484 items, so the proxy is what runs everywhere else. effort is also an optional gated field, present on 0 items, and it is read where present and nowhere else: unlike deadline it has NO proxy to fall back on, so an item without one is scored on the other criteria, renormalized, rather than guessed at. links is empty on 247 of 484 items. Where an input was missing, the score carries a LOW-CONFIDENCE flag naming it. A clean-looking number from thin data is the one thing this page must never print.

How much this ranking actually separates the work: not much, and here is the number. The 417 scored items span 5 to 69 out of 100 across 53 distinct values, so the gap between rank 1 and rank 417 is 64 points. That is what a ranking looks like when its heaviest criterion has no data: the order below is a tie-break, not a verdict. It gets sharper the moment links exists.

What the missing-input words in the last column mean. age — opened is 0 — the item's birth session is unknown, so cost-of-delay cannot be measured. deadline — the item has no `deadline` field — the score reads a prose scan of the item's own words as a PROXY, so an item scores higher by SAYING 'friday'.

Flagged problem — the dependency graph does not exist

237 of 484 items carry links — 49%

Among the 417 live items it is 210. links is a required field, so it is present on every item — and empty on almost all of them. The consequence is concrete and it is on this page: the heaviest criterion in the score above (Unlock, weight 0.353) has nothing to read, contributes zero to every item, and the remaining 0.647 of weight is doing all the work of ranking your backlog.

Queued as your work for the Friday 2026-08-14 set, not repaired here. I could populate links by guessing which item blocks which from the prose — and then the score would rank your backlog against a dependency graph I invented and you never saw. That is a closed loop with no reader in it. The number is reported; the backfill waits for you.

SOURCE · knowledge/_state.json, counted at generation. This panel moves on its own the moment links are written — it is a measurement, not a note.

Board — quick visual reference

484 items, four columns

The columns are DERIVED, not stored. _state.json has no status, lane, phase or priority field — the only lifecycle axes that exist in the data are state (open / blocked / done) and condition (UNCONDITIONED / stated, i.e. whether a closes_when is set). project IS stored (you ruled it at #172) — it is a grouping label, not a lifecycle, so it makes a filter and not a column. Each column head prints the exact rule that put items in it, so you can check the derivation rather than trust it. Owner is the store’s owner, written as a WORD; where the store marks it inferred the card says inferred. Cards are ordered by the proposed priority score (an overruled card sits at the top of its column); the Done column stays in id order, because a finished thing has no priority.

The two plates

What is on your plate, and what is on mine

Owner comes from _state.json. Where the store marks the owner as inferred, the item says so — an inference is not a ruling. Both plates are in proposed-priority order, highest score first, with any item you have overruled lifted to the top of its plate; the badge on each item says which of the two it is.

How to read this page

Generated by knowledge/gen_dashboard.py from the stores; it is never hand-edited. --check regenerates and compares, so a stale dashboard fails the build rather than lying quietly. It carries no timestamp on purpose: its clock is the session number in _CHAIN.md (#245) and the measurements themselves.

Components are Mono, from knowledge/canon/canon.css — nothing new was invented. Layout follows the swiss-design-system skill. Every verdict is a WORD first; the two-red law (red #DA1A00) and its green mirror (#137F3C) are applied on white, light mode only, as redundant confirmation. The blue rules and dashes are decorative and carry no meaning.

This page reports. It does not repair, rule, or promote anything.
